## Goal

Replace the discarded block/background conversion direction with jointly authored, rights-clean 3D gameplay models, a perspective-defining track, and one locally owned test environment. Preserve PlayCanvas as the single runtime renderer and keep all assets self-contained.

## Approved visual direction to plan

- **Directional note:** DDR-style 3D arrow with a white outline treatment, shared by Flow and Boxing.
- **Any note:** circular 3D note with the same coherent visual language.
- **Guard:** one canonical shield model/icon reused identically for both hands. A guard beat displays two simultaneous instances—one in each applicable left/right grid cell or lane—so both hands see the same shield at once. “Shared” means shared geometry/icon identity, not a single displayed instance; do not distinguish the two shields with separate geometry.
- **Athlete markers:** replace flat 2D-looking nose and wrist circles with full 3D spheres placed at the truthful nose/wrist world positions. Preserve existing role colors and occlusion/depth behavior.
- **Timing rows and beat tint:** keep the near-athlete grid rows colored red/yellow/green. As an approaching beat enters the success area, tint its 3D model white to communicate timing without replacing its silhouette.
- **Hit/miss feedback:** on threshold resolution, remove the beat quickly and spawn short-lived world-space text at the beat’s crossing position and height. Successful hits show `Great` in white; misses show `Miss` in red with a white outline. Feedback must remain only briefly and must not distract or obscure later beats.
- **Wall obstacle:** semi-transparent red rectangular volume. In Flow its world-Z length must span the complete authoritative obstacle interval rather than appearing as a short temporary slab.
- **Bomb:** black sea-urchin silhouette—a sphere with sharp cone spines—with red emissive glow/outline signaling avoidance.
- **Track:** semi-transparent blue-tinted glass surface below the athlete and event lanes, extending through the visible approach path so camera perspective and beat travel are immediately legible.
- **Test environment:** use the already-owned local GLB at `/home/derrick/.dsh/projects/aerobeat/aerobeat-environment-community/.testbed/assets/models/alien-moon-icescape/alien-moon-icescape.glb` with its sibling transform config. Do not acquire or redistribute third-party assets.
- **Future environments:** locally owned Gaussian splats remain a later explicitly approved experiment, not part of this first implementation.

## Constraints

- Handcrafted/model-generation work must be reviewed visually by Derrick before runtime integration.
- Use one canonical asset per semantic role where specified; do not duplicate left/right shield geometry.
- White outlines must remain visible against the ice environment and transparent track without breaking depth ordering or alpha.
- Translucent walls and track must retain truthful depth, interval length, and gameplay visibility.
- Preserve role colors, handedness, timing, scoring, event IDs, camera conventions, privacy, and public contracts.
- No Theme runtime, network asset fetch, public release, upload, third-party acquisition, or Gaussian-splat integration in this slice.

## Approved future asset ownership and packaging direction

- Keep the canonical gameplay art in its own cross-engine polyrepo named `aerobeat-asset-gameplay`, following the separation used by the Godot asset lane. Use `aerobeat-web-asset-gameplay` only if the payload later gains web-specific assets or contracts.
- Preserve engine-neutral ownership: editable Blender/source files, provenance, export tooling, validation, individual GLBs, and mix-and-match set manifests belong to the asset polyrepo; PlayCanvas/Godot consumers own only engine integration and rendering behavior.
- Keep every swappable semantic role in a separate editable source directory and separate GLB rather than a combined gameplay GLB. Initial roles are directional arrow, any-note circle, guard shield, bomb, normalized wall, track, and shared athlete-marker sphere.
- Name the initial directional-arrow variation `outline-v1`; do not encode the inspiration source in the asset identifier.
- A set manifest selects one versioned variation independently per role so complete sets and arbitrary combinations can coexist. The shield GLB remains one canonical asset instantiated twice for a guard event.
- `Great`/`Miss` remains renderer-owned world text rather than a GLB. Dynamic interval scaling, timing tint, row colors, instancing, outline passes, and scoring/timing behavior remain consumer/runtime responsibilities.
- Future immutable asset releases should preserve separate GLBs, manifests, per-file hashes, coordinate/pivot/bounds metadata, provenance, and export-tool versions. A consuming assembly pins an exact asset release and copies only its selected assets into that assembly's immutable release.
- This section originally approved architecture and naming only; the later explicit execution approval below now authorizes Tasks 2–6 and creation of the public asset polyrepo while preserving all listed ownership boundaries.

### Task 2 — Create canonical self-contained model assets

- If separately approved, create the cross-engine `aerobeat-asset-gameplay` polyrepo; use `aerobeat-web-asset-gameplay` instead only if concrete web-specific payload becomes necessary.
- Generate or model each role as an independently swappable source/export pair: directional arrow variation `outline-v1`, any-note circle, shield, bomb, normalized wall, track, and shared athlete-marker sphere.
- Keep one separate GLB per role/variation and author mix-and-match set manifests; do not combine all gameplay models into one GLB.
- Author red translucent wall and blue glass track materials while leaving runtime scaling, tint, instancing, outline passes, and world text to engine consumers.
- Normalize transforms and deterministic source/export files; record provenance, rights ownership, per-file hashes, bounds, pivots, and coordinate metadata for every asset.

### Task 3 — Add renderer-owned 3D asset contract

- Load canonical local assets through the renderer without introducing runtime network dependencies.
- Map Flow and Boxing directional events to the shared arrow, directionless events to the circle, each guard event to two simultaneous instances of the one canonical shield in both applicable cells/lanes, obstacles to interval-sized walls, and bombs to the urchin model.
- Replace flat-looking nose/wrist markers with depth-correct 3D spheres; add success-area white beat tint and threshold-positioned short-lived `Great`/`Miss` world feedback without changing timing/scoring truth.
- Keep fallback behavior explicit and testable during development; remove obsolete production sprite/block paths when landing.

### Task 4 — Correct full-length Flow walls and add the glass track

- Derive wall center and world-Z scale from authoritative `centerTimestampMs → endTimestampMs` interval truth.
- Add a stable blue-glass approach track below the athlete/grid and through the visible timeline depth.
- Preserve red/yellow/green near-athlete row bands while ensuring the white success-area beat tint remains distinct from the white-outlined track and environment.
- Validate sorting, clipping, transparency, perspective, 3D athlete-marker occlusion, and short-lived feedback readability at supported viewports/DPRs.

### Task 5 — Integrate alien moon ice test environment

- Import the local GLB and sibling transform config as a self-contained test background.
- Establish camera/environment/track framing without changing gameplay coordinates.
- Keep environment selection and lifecycle teardown deterministic.

### Task 6 — QA, audit, and physical review

- Unit/model tests for semantic mapping, reuse, full obstacle length, transforms, and deterministic manifests.
- Chromium direct/iframe portrait/landscape DPR matrix for depth, alpha, outlines, handedness, and full-length walls.
- Visual evidence for Flow, Boxing Lanes, and Boxing Grid.
- Derrick physical review before immutable successor release work.

## Bead dependency-direction diagnosis and repair (2026-09-03)

- `k72.12` initially required forced closure because the initial graph used `bd create --deps blocks:<predecessor>` as though it meant “depends on predecessor”; it actually made each newly created task block its predecessor. This reversed the execution chain and masked readiness because agents were assigned explicitly.
- Full diagnosis is preserved in `.plans/debug/2026-09-03-bead-dependency-direction.md`. Every erroneous `blocks` edge was removed and re-added with explicit `bd dep add <successor> <predecessor>` syntax. Parent-child and discovered-from links were preserved.
- `bd dep cycles --json` returns `[]`; `k72.13` is now the sole ready execution task, while `k72.14` → `k72.15` → `k72.16` → `k72.17` are correctly blocked in order. Renderer parent `k72.11` then closed normally without force because its implementation, visual, and QA prerequisites are complete.

### Photosphere catalog/control contract for `k72.28`

- Renderer accepts a bounded assembly-owned equirectangular descriptor generically rather than hard-coding one JPEG identity: lowercase asset id, package-local same-origin URL, JPEG MIME, bounded positive bytes, exact SHA-256, RGB `4096×2048`, projection, center-forward `−Z`, world-up `+Y`, and strict transform config. Assembly owns the exact eight-entry hash catalog and Vite emissions; renderer remains payload-free.
- One JSON config belongs to exactly one environment id. Schema fields are version/id/projection plus `transform.position`, `transform.rotationDegrees`, and `transform.scale`; vectors are three finite numbers, scale is positive/bounded, and load rejects extra keys or mismatched selected id. The approved physical-revision instruction supersedes the earlier schema-only position note: Photosphere UI exposes bounded position X/Y/Z, yaw/pitch/roll, and uniform sphere-radius scale controls.
- The photosphere node stays camera-anchored at the approved gameplay camera origin plus configured local position. Rotation applies pitch/X, yaw/Y, roll/Z. Uniform scale changes the bounded sphere radius (minimum keeps the camera inside); the UI must truthfully avoid promising zoom when a centered inside-sphere scale has no angular effect.
- Selection and transforms are per-instance private Visual Test state. Save is an explicit-user-action JSON download; load uses an explicit local file picker and strict parse/validation. Neither file bytes, selection, transform, URL, hash, nor catalog extends host events, iframe messages, public snapshots, localStorage, or network fetch. Switching replaces one verified texture generation-safely; no eight-texture GPU cache is allowed on mobile.
- A 44px-accessible chevron collapses/reopens the complete top-left Visual Test control surface without disconnecting renderer/CV/audio or changing gameplay state. Default is open; collapse state is per connected instance and does not alter environment configuration.
- Architecture research `k72.33` completed PASS with no code changes. The full Bead comment maps the renderer owner/facade/decision/validators and assembly catalog/index/Vite/sync/component/shell/privacy validators. It confirms strict `aerobeat/environment_asset_config` v1 JSON, trusted explicit-user-action files bounded to 16 KiB, one resident texture, stale-generation abort/disposal, gradient fallback on replacement failure, camera-translation anchoring without camera-rotation inheritance, transform-before-descriptor ordering, per-connection per-id memory, computed Camera mode, and reconnect/context/destroy/multi-instance tests. It explicitly preserves the truth that centered sphere scale changes radius but not angular zoom. Bead `k72.33` is closed and pushed; implementation `k72.28` remains gated by the three active asset/grid/environment lanes.
- Square-grid diagnosis is complete before implementation in `.plans/debug/2026-09-03-square-grid-collider-mismatch.md`: all 16 raw/source, direct/iframe, portrait/landscape, DPR1/3 cases reproduce the same `1.5×1.05` cell (`1.4286` ratio), while the approved wall GLB is near-square `1.8×1.9`. The scoped implementation replaces those literals with a single equal-unit 4×3 transform and derives square inset cells/cursors from it while preserving cell IDs, scoring/timing, camera, wall asset and interval truth. ## Derrick physical review of raw `0.0.36` — FAIL; `i4u` diagnosis complete (2026-09-03)

- Derrick's remote Tailscale HTTP review failed: DDR and other BeatSaver downloads show exact error `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'digest')`. Physical gate `k72.17` remains FAIL/in progress; bug `aerobeat-web-assembly-i4u` owns recovery. Raw `0.0.36` remains immutable and the managed `100.113.165.57:5173` server remains unchanged as failed physical evidence.
- Diagnostic Chromium reproduced the exact UI action path on `http://100.113.165.57:5173/`: `isSecureContext=false`, `globalThis.crypto` exists, and `crypto.subtle` is undefined. The archive request succeeded (`200`, `2,309,957` bytes), then raw source-map evidence resolved the cause to BeatSaver `archive.js:79` SHA-1. A temporary localhost control serving identical bytes reported secure context/WebCrypto available and successfully authored three packages, loaded content/audio, all seven gameplay GLBs, and the environment; that temporary server was stopped.
- Scope is broader than the first song error. Content authoring (including its Worker), runtime package/assets, encoded audio, renderer GLBs, and environment JPEGs all depend on `SubtleCrypto.digest`; renderer gameplay assets and the photosphere already fail verification on the insecure origin before download. ## `b9e` BeatSaver explicit metadata-version fallback diagnosis (2026-09-03)

- Fresh public-inspector reproduction on exact clean vendor candidate `a654d91934186d4ea2c00923c9d8d0700d29737b` confirmed that explicit `version: "5.0.0"` and `"1.0.0"`, wrong-type/empty/malformed declarations, permissive numeric prefixes, and conflicting `version`/`_version` pairs are accepted as v2/v3/v4 through shape fallback. The same explicit v5 archive succeeds through provider download and local import because both paths share `inspectBeatSaverArchive()`.
- Full diagnosis and exact matrix are preserved in `.plans/debug/2026-09-03-beatsaver-explicit-version-fallback.md`. `buildSourceManifest()` currently erases present-invalid values through `optionalString()`, while unchanged-since-`938c6f55` `detectFormatMajor()` uses permissive `parseInt` then falls into legacy shape inference. README policy has always promised unsupported metadata rejection; existing tests cover only valid declared v2/v3/v4 and ZIP-security failures.
- Required minimal repair is to validate every own `version`/`_version` declaration as a complete supported version, reject any malformed/unsupported declaration or cross-major dual-key conflict with stable `unsupported`, and invoke the existing v2/v3/v4 shape heuristic only when both declaration keys are truly absent. Preserve supported key aliases, same-major dual declarations, hybrid legacy shape tolerance, exact provider hash semantics, and documented versionless compatibility; do not delete heuristics or add broad shape-coherence rejection.
- JSON parsing means inherited/accessor/proxy object identity cannot reach the detector: the public boundary receives bytes and native `JSON.parse()` creates ordinary own data properties. Tests must lock serialization/parse reality (`__proto__` does not mutate the prototype; serialized accessor/proxy values become ordinary declarations) rather than inventing an unreachable direct-object API.
- Diagnosis changed only this plan and the persistent debug report. No vendor/authoring/shared product, test, package, release, server, commit, push, or closure action occurred. `b9e` remains in progress for the parent/coder repair and full `i4u.1.2` regression matrix.

## Raw `0.0.37` physical-defect diagnosis — COMPLETE; no implementation (2026-09-04)

- **Arrow is not alpha blended.** Exact raw/source GLB JSON and live PlayCanvas materials are opacity `1`, `BLEND_NONE (3)`, `depthWrite=true`, and `depthTest=true`. A controlled raw-bundle black/white oracle found `4,212` invariant interior pixels and center RGBA `46,54,66,255`; only `277` antialiased edge pixels varied with the background. The intentional resolved-note removal fade remains a separate bounded `80 ms` behavior.
- **Arrow root cause is compound grid occlusion plus wrong-side geometry and low-contrast tint.** A refined exact-raw Chromium probe placed the pending opaque arrow at `z=-1.5`, behind camera-nearer transparent grid cells at `z=0`: enabling the grid altered all `1,381/1,381` sampled arrow pixels with mean RGB shift `114.08` (`[46,54,66,255] → [86,92,102,255]`) while alpha stayed `255`. At crossing `z=0`, `2,147/2,364` arrow pixels remained unchanged because the front cap moved ahead of most grid geometry. Separately, asset release `0.0.3` retained the exact `1,100`-byte geometry BIN from `0.0.2`; the attempted fix only made already-implicit OPAQUE metadata explicit and renamed cyan to `tint_base`. `rear_and_sides()` and `extrude(inset)` generate two coplanar rear caps at runtime-facing `z=+0.09` (`5+5` overlapping triangles), while the clean white-rimmed face is authored for `-Z`. The runtime camera sits at `+Z`; review generation places its camera at `-Z`. Near crossing, `tintMix=0.6667` shifts the remaining runtime-facing surface toward pale white. Existing checks prove metadata/material alpha and nominal draw order but not grid-on/grid-off target occlusion, structural non-overlap, runtime-facing contrast, or per-arrow pixels. Bead `aerobeat-web-assembly-10c` contains both debugging records.
- **Recommended arrow correction:** prevent transparent cells/grid from compositing in front of approaching targets through corrected depth placement or explicit target/grid layering, and source-first re-author `directional-arrow/outline-v1` with closed, styled, non-coplanar opaque geometry on both camera-accessible faces—or at minimum correct the `+Z` runtime-facing face—while retaining base alpha `1`, OPAQUE blending, and depth writes. Prefer an asset correction over a renderer `Y=180°` flip, which risks left/right direction mirroring. Add a GLB structural assertion forbidding coplanar caps, grid-on/grid-off target pixel checks, `+Z/-Z` bright-environment captures, per-arrow black/white alpha invariance, contrast thresholds, every direction/mode/free-fly case, and explicit removal-progress tests.
- **Transform labels:** `applyEnvironmentControls()` updates native values, private config, and renderer synchronously, then calls `renderEnvironmentControls(false)`, which deliberately skips `<output>` projection. Reset/Play later call the default `true` path, creating the delayed-label symptom. The degree glyph is a fourth direct child auto-placed into row two of a three-column grid. Correct with immediate output projection and one no-wrap value/unit wrapper with explicit accessible input/output association. Remove the two requested strings while explicitly clearing stale success status.
- **Alpine authority:** Derrick's supplied `/home/derrick/Downloads/alpine-river-valley-photosphere.environment-config.v1.json` is strict deterministic v1 data, `336` bytes, SHA-256 `69a62f73bc1193b0e9414c2698a269e24ed06c309616dc345a3f5cf80966f977`; its sole semantic difference from the `334`-byte current config (`c1cda19a…`) is Alpine yaw `0 → 180`. Promote it source-first through `aerobeat-environment-community` generator/validator/manifest/catalog aggregate, then repin/sync assembly and make per-entry packaged transforms runtime authority. Set Alpine as fresh/reconnect default without reordering the eight-entry catalog or overwriting a connection-local user selection; do not add browser persistence. Decouple environment classification from default identity.
- **Secure review:** the historical Tailscale Serve route never disappeared. Tailnet-only `https://derrick-alienware-aurora-r13.tail613fcb.ts.net:8443/` already proxies `/` to `http://127.0.0.1:5173`, serves exact raw `0.0.37` bytes, has a valid MagicDNS certificate, reports `isSecureContext=true`, exposes callable `getUserMedia`, and leaves camera permission at `prompt`. The mistake was binding the backend broadly at `0.0.0.0`; implementation should leave Tailscale Serve and unrelated `:8444` untouched, replace only managed job `bash-22` with the same absolute immutable raw root bound to `127.0.0.1`, then re-audit all `41` files, HTTPS/certificate/camera state, one listener/process, tailnet-only status, and direct `:5173` refusal. Bead `aerobeat-web-assembly-0mx` contains the exact switch and rollback procedure.
- No source, asset, bundle, raw release, server, or Tailscale state was changed during diagnosis. ## Source-faithful Flow obstacle gameplay design — PASS / IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORIZED (2026-09-04)

- Research/design child `133b12b5-3547-4bbb-9bd5-59d1c3d05c97` completed `h51` read-only, recorded the complete actionable design as Bead comment `01a06e16-4870-7677-87f0-67dce407f8b7`, closed only `h51` PASS, and pushed Dolt. Parent verified the closure, comment, cycle-free graph, and that `v5q` is now blocked only by the closed design prerequisite. The child edited no source, plan, server, or immutable release; its expected tracked closure event is included with this plan update.
- New source authority is exact `aerobeat/flow_obstacle_geometry` v1 `{schema,version,coordinateSpace:"beatsaber_lane_layer",x,y,width,height}`. New Flow/package/resolved-event v2 contracts replace ambiguous obstacle `cells` with continuous `geometry` plus a unique sorted derived `gridMask`; the mask is bounded UI/indexing/Boxing feasibility truth only and may never drive Flow rendering or collision. Legacy v1 remains list/export/delete compatible but is not silently reinterpreted: Play/Test requires atomic reimport and reports `flow_obstacle_reimport_required`; persistence migrates DB `4→5` by labeling legacy rows without rewriting stored package bytes or hashes.
- Normalization is fail-closed and source-specific: v2 type `0→y=0,height=5`, type `1→y=2,height=3`; v3 uses inline `b/d/x/y/w/h`; v4 resolves indexed `obstaclesData`; malformed indexes, ranges, duration, conflicting geometry, unsupported type, and nonzero/unsupported rotation reject atomically rather than clamp/default. The exact `3c9d` Hard golden is provider hash `5662f64a12c76a3dd11a5f6ee22611608cd06760`, BPM `150`, beat `92.5999984741211→92.6624984741211`, `37039.99938964844→37064.99938964844 ms`, geometry `{x:1,y:2,width:1,height:3}`, derived mask `[1]`. It renders one continuous wall centered `(-0.5,3)`, visual face `0.94×2.94`, interval Z `0.15`; it must never become one square or fake cells `[1,5,9]`.
- Collision uses only measured calibrated nose as the available head proxy; wrists, predicted pose, retained render geometry, and video pixels never collide. Nose maps to logical source space as `sx=4*nx-0.5`, `sy=2.5-3*ny`. Consecutive valid same-calibration measurements are analytically clipped through obstacle time and logical rectangle so the real `25 ms` wall remains detectable at `15 fps`; no interpolation crosses stale/lost/invalid/rollback/duplicate/calibration-change evidence or gaps above `150 ms`. Tracking uncertainty produces non-penalizing `unevaluated_tracking`, not a guessed collision or avoidance.
- Overlapping contacts aggregate into deterministic entry episodes. Aggregate clear→occupied resets combo exactly once, applies zero direct points, leaves note hit/miss/ignored arithmetic and max-combo history unchanged, and records bounded source obstacle outcomes (`contact`, `avoided`, `unevaluated_tracking`) without exposing pose coordinates/frame/calibration IDs publicly. Continuous overlap does not repeat consequence; leaving and re-entering creates another deterministic episode. Contact is not a note miss and emits no `Great`/`Miss`; a deterministic wall pulse and accessible `Obstacle hit` status are allowed. `visual_test` is visual-only. Energy/fail/multiplier-tier/audio-muffling constants are explicitly excluded rather than guessed.
- Default mode is visual plus head collision. Between-run identity-bound unranked/local-only accessibility may support `no_obstacles` and optional `obstacle_visual_only`; modifiers never mutate authored package bytes. Renderer transform is one continuous instance: `centerX=x+(width-1)/2-1.5`, `centerY=y+(height-1)/2`, visual width/height `width-0.06` / `height-0.06`, X/Y scale divided by source `0.94`, exact interval-derived Z with no minimum-depth distortion. Collision uses exact logical bounds while visuals retain the `0.03` outer inset. Shadows use the continuous footprint but remain renderer-only and non-colliding.

### Derrick's contract clarification

> “we're trying to match the contract beatsaver gives us. The obstacles in the DDR song map appear in the top row, and extend down to the bottom row. Our older contract was single cell only, which was incorrect. Our updated contract was multi cell is allowed, however the obstacles in the same timestamp we're testing against are appearing above the within the top row and above the cell grid, not taking up the middle and bottom rows to match the beatsaver arcviewer app. So our contract is either incorrect or not being enforced correctly.”

### Corrected diagnosis

- The updated contract is enforced consistently, but its vertical normalization is incorrect for required BeatSaver/ArcViewer parity. Exact `3c9d` Hard v2 type `1` currently normalizes to internal `{x:1,y:2,width:1,height:3}` and incorrectly carries the bottom-origin/upward interpretation into AeroBeat world space. This yields `[1]`, renderer center `(-0.5,3)`, dimensions `0.94×2.94×0.15`, and top-only collision Y `1.5..4.5`.
- No facade, GLB pivot, opacity, overlay, or hidden-lower-wall defect exists. The renderer and collision engine faithfully enforce the wrong normalized contract.
- The correct external-compatibility footprint is top-row anchored and extends downward through the canonical grid: `[1,5,9]`, center `(-0.5,1)`, Y AABB `-0.47..2.47`, with aligned all-row calibrated-nose collision.
- Prior plan/test language calling `[1,5,9]` fake or a product adaptation is superseded. QA/audit previously proved internal consistency rather than the physically observed ArcViewer footprint.
- Full corrected debug record: `.plans/debug/2026-09-05-raw-0.0.39-obstacle-vertical-occupancy.md`.

1. Version the Flow obstacle record and correct the coordinate-space boundary rather than silently reusing v1 `beatsaber_lane_layer` geometry as canonical gameplay geometry:

4. Bump chart/package/resolved-event/persistence provenance monotonically. Existing prior-contract stored packages remain list/export/delete-only and require reimport for Play/Test. DB migration labels provenance only; never rewrite stored package bytes/hashes.
5. Replace incorrect `[1]`/center-Y-`3`/top-only acceptance locks with exact same-timestamp source→normalized mask→collision→scene AABB→direct/iframe pixel parity. Update contracts, authoring, content, gameplay, renderer, UI/assembly, docs, and fixtures.

## Terminal lifecycle `4in` — CODER PASS / QA READY (2026-09-05)

- Established one exact lifecycle contract across gameplay, audio, and assembly. Gameplay `completed` is terminal between-run truth: ordinary pause/menu/visibility/lease paths cannot convert it to `paused_manual`; only an explicit stopped-clock seek may establish a precise paused timeline with `pauseReason: explicit_seek`. Audio natural end remains `stopped@duration` while its lease is released, and production `play()@duration` retains its documented rewind-to-zero behavior.
- Assembly now publishes explicit menu disposition `active-paused | terminal | none`. `menuPauseArmed` is a read gate that becomes true only after the active gameplay/audio/CV pause settles and is consumed on close. Terminal open calls no gameplay pause. Terminal close starts no audio, gameplay resume, CV, video, or media lease—even when menu song/difficulty/mode/variant/scoring changes configure the next run into calibration truth.
- Terminal reconciliation runs once per assembly session generation: stop display/debug motion, pause scored-Play audio if still running, stop CV, pause retained video presentation, release the exact lease, then publish only if connection/session identity is still current. Explicit Start/Test invalidates stale menu callbacks and awaits the prior menu pause, Visual Test transport queue (unless already executing that serialized intent), and terminal reconciliation before stopping old media, seeking zero, configuring exact selected content, requesting a fresh gameplay generation, clearing prior outcomes, and reacquiring camera+audio for Play or audio-only for Test.
- Visual Test backward seek from completion now yields an exact frozen `paused_manual` timeline. - Initial real-camera local profiling proved synchronous main-thread GPU MediaPipe as the missed-frame cause (`175.1–215.4 ms` runtime p95; roughly `4.4–5.4 FPS` display). GPU-worker VideoFrame was rejected for second-WebGL/inference and 500 ms freshness regressions. CPU-WASM worker VideoFrame materially improved paired display from `4.00/4.31` to `22.42/27.64 FPS`, with max pose age `454.0/326.3 ms`; production therefore uses one in-flight, latest-frame admission, exact measured timestamp, guaranteed-close worker execution with measured-current output only.

Staged B/A was `99.2%`; visible Camera was not slower, so no compositor repair shipped. CV was `6.23–7.47 FPS`, pose-age p95 `247.3–282.4 ms`, max `277.4–320.0 ms`. Capture remains unconstrained because the browser already selected `640×480@30` and no pose/calibration accuracy evidence supports imposing it.
- Full renderer and MediaPipe vendor unit/browser/dry-pack gates passed. Assembly unit, privacy, immutable raw, complete direct/iframe orientation/DPR mode matrix, camera/compositor/cursor/square-grid/visual correction, live marker, exact Flow/four-Boxing obstacles, terminal lifecycle, and dry-pack gates passed. The aggregate browser wrapper hit its 10-minute cap after visual correction; remaining live marker passed separately. One accumulated-load terminal wait timed out once and passed immediately in isolation; no speculative timeout edit was made.
- Local stress display remained `33.88–36.61 FPS`, below the `>=45` target. After release audit, secure successor gate `k72.17` performs the physical Bug A/B/C/C/B/A profile and requires `>=45 FPS` where supported, `>=90%` camera-on/Aero rate, `<=15.01` CV FPS, existing 500 ms fail-closed safety, calibration invalidation, denial/error teardown, privacy, and no prediction/fabrication. ## DB7 cancellation/first-error race diagnosis — QA FAIL, report only (2026-09-06)

- Fresh independent callback audit confirmed the `9cd5136` repair bounds the originally reported delete, conditional-delete, GC, nested-legacy, migration, shared-asset, and completion-time resolver callbacks, but found an untested competing-abort gap in `putIndexedDbCollection`.
- Deterministic fake-IDB diagnosis ordered a hostile current-DB7 package decoder failure before a supplied signal abort. The guarded decoder first recorded `storage_record_invalid / Stored package record shape is invalid` and called `tx.abort()`. Before `tx.onabort` delivery, the signal aborted; the raw listener called `tx.abort()` again, leaked uncaught `InvalidStateError`, and `tx.onabort` reported `operation_aborted / Persistence operation was cancelled` because it tests mutable `signal.aborted` before `transactionStorageError`. Observed abort count was two; rollback remained atomic.
- Confirmed root cause: abort causality is inferred from signal state at terminal-event time rather than latched when the first abort cause occurs, and the signal listener is neither idempotent nor exception-safe. Cancellation-first must remain `operation_aborted`; decoder-first must preserve the exact original storage error; cancellation after completion/listener cleanup must be inert. Exact real-Chromium second-abort/noise behavior remains an explicit verification unknown.
- Persistent diagnosis with the complete debug-skill sections, ordering matrix, browser/fake-IDB distinction, minimal reproduction, verification plan, and smallest recommended repair is `.plans/debug/2026-09-06-db7-cancellation-first-error-race.md`.
- Linked P1 blocker `aerobeat-web-assembly-8ik` blocks `2q6`; `570`, `htd`, and `n4x` remain open. This slice changes diagnosis/report/plan evidence only: no authoring product/runtime/test, assembly runtime/test, raw release, serving, publication, tag, GitHub Release, or physical claim is changed. Release authorization remains **NO**.

## Resume-path exact-generation rollback — CODER PASS / CONSOLIDATED RE-QA READY (2026-09-07)

- Parent source audit created P0 `w07r`, blocking `hxxs`, after finding ordinary Play `resume()` and Visual Test transport resume shared the same uncovered post-acquisition stale-return shape. Both could acquire resources then lose connection/session/visibility ownership; transport could also resume gameplay before `audio.play()` failed or became stale. Gameplay's copied lease snapshot could remain inconsistent with the global coordinator after compensation.
- The `hxxs` helper is generalized rather than duplicated. `requestActionResources()` renews a serialized exact generation even for a same-participant/same-resource request without duplicate activation callbacks. Start/Test, public resume, and transport resume bind to that identity and compensate only while uncommitted. Exact-generation release cannot touch a later action; current gameplay receives the truthful resulting coordinator snapshot. Play resume retains its pre-existing camera stream; transport failure/staleness stops audio, pauses resumed gameplay, and preserves the first error.

## Raw `0.0.43` physical feedback repair authorization (2026-09-07)

Derrick physically reviewed the exact securely served candidate and authorized immediate source work on three consolidated failures/features while continuing to reserve final physical PASS:

1. **Live bounce and configurable approach (`xz09`).** Default and maximum-obvious bounce settings (`8` lead beats, `1.5` world-unit height) produced no visible bounce, so this is a live integration failure rather than a tuning preference. Diagnose the exact authored timing → private projection → renderer path before repair. Replace ad-hoc distance constants with one private, branded, strict-JSON Test authoring contract. It must expose the normal far-lane spawn distance plus an optional sky pre-entry: the sky path begins farther away/elevated, joins the assigned canonical lane exactly at the normal far-distance visibility boundary, then follows the unchanged lane approach to authoritative hit time. Derrick must be able to tune, export, and return the exact deterministic JSON for default promotion. Bounce remains presentation-only, hittable-only, additive, absolute-time, and exact-hit landing; bombs/walls/squat/weave stay static.
2. **Uniform rounded arrow source (`s54e`).** The retained charcoal→white→charcoal→fill hierarchy is desired, but physical pixels show uneven inner keyline width at the arrow's concave shoulders/tips. Rebuild the directional arrow source using genuinely uniform rounded exterior and interior signed offsets; preserve the circle unless evidence requires otherwise. Immutable asset `0.0.8` must never change. Stage source/manifest evidence, QA/audit it, then authorize exactly one later immutable gameplay-asset successor only after all feedback is consolidated.
3. **Boxing Lanes geometry and separation (`1r2t`).** Physical Boxing Lanes walls occupy only approximately the center-row height; every continuous obstacle must instead span the complete top-through-bottom vertical lane for its exact interval. Expose bounded center-to-center lane separation through the same private Test authoring/save/load surface, without changing lane width, hand semantics, timing, collision, scoring, canonical grid truth, or public/privacy contracts. Derrick must be able to tune/export this value for default promotion.

Execution is coder → independent QA → final audit across renderer, asset source, and assembly. Do not mutate/rebuild raw `0.0.42`, raw `0.0.43`, or gameplay asset `0.0.8`; do not build any new immutable asset/web target until source integration audit explicitly authorizes exactly one successor. ## Raw `0.0.46` physical review — REJECTED / CONCEPT DISCUSSION BEFORE CODE (`pver`, 2026-09-08)

- Derrick physically rejected the securely served immutable raw. Required defaults: Boxing Lanes separation becomes `1.8`; Flow and Boxing Grid sky prelude height becomes `50`.
- In all three gameplay types, the continuous same-ID gray miss repair removed the disappear/reappear gap as intended, but the frozen miss is not the desired final behavior. After graying at the crossing, the same missed beat must continue traveling on/down the track as a visibly missed target before its bounded expiry. The `Miss` label must render above rather than below the gray beat.
- Reentrant transport is physically broken: pressing Test from the open menu while a Test is active unloads the song without reloading; pressing Play while already playing similarly enters a bad state. The accepted contract must choose and consistently implement either an intentional restart/reload or an explicit no-op; an unloaded/corrupt intermediate state is forbidden.
- Derrick requested concept discussion before any code: (1) improve attention order for dense nearby timing beyond shadows and bounce; (2) explore bounded horizontal/vertical gameplay-camera parallax driven by the active webcam nose landmark; (3) determine whether BeatSaver exposes song/difficulty-specific spawn-range data similar to Beat Bat.
- BeatSaver research confirms `versions[].diffs[]` API rows expose `njs` and `offset`, while the underlying Info.dat difficulty row exposes `noteJumpMovementSpeed` / `noteJumpStartBeatOffset`. Live API read-back for `3c9d` returned Easy `6/1`, Normal `8/1`, Hard `10/1`, Expert `11/1`, ExpertPlus `17/1` for NJS/offset. The local vendor archive parser already retains both exact Info.dat values in `aerobeat.beatsaver-source-manifest.v2`, but `selectedDifficultyMetadata()` deliberately narrows the authoring request and drops both before content-package persistence. Runtime therefore cannot yet consume this authority without an explicit source→authoring→package contract extension.
- P0 umbrella `aerobeat-web-assembly-pver` was created/claimed from `k72.17` and blocks that physical gate. Durable feedback/research evidence is comment `01a0839b-9b37-7295-bab1-4d0193502fbc`. No code, release bytes, serving route, or physical-PASS state changed.
- Derrick selected an experimental timing run with the discussed cues independently toggleable for physical comparison; full gameplay-camera parallax with corrected input/marker projection; direct Beat Saber spawn mapping by default plus an explicit enable/disable AeroBeat override; and an always-controlled fresh restart for every Play/Test action.
- Research Beads `qm2r` (toggleable timing cues and full nose-camera design) and `dlai` (defaults/miss/transport diagnosis plus exact Beat Saber mapping) were created as true blockers of `pver`. Standalone research children `1dcc0993-7d7f-43e6-8ac1-f060a24d32d4` and `a6cdd37a-870b-40a4-9ab8-234334c82d71` were launched and verified running. ## Proposed third-feedback execution addendum — AWAITING DERRICK APPROVAL

### Confirmed architecture and contracts

- **Mode defaults:** replace the stale renderer-owned Boxing Lanes separation `2.7` with `1.8`, producing lane centers `±0.9`. Derrick explicitly approved sky-prelude height `50` for Flow, Boxing Grid, and Boxing Lanes. Fresh graph, reconnect, reset, direct/iframe, and strict JSON controls must agree.
- **Continuous moving miss:** preserve the same event ID and pending target at `Z=0` through the inclusive `+180 ms` late-hit boundary. Pending post-crossing remains the authored song color rather than the current white success tint. At `+181 ms`, that same ID atomically becomes gray at `Z=0`, then advances in `+Z` at the canonical `.006 world units/ms` for the existing bounded `350 ms` feedback life and expires once. Its icon, shadow, and billboarded `Miss` label travel together; the label anchors above the target with bounded clearance. Hits retain their existing crossing/`80 ms` behavior; scoring truth is unchanged.
- **Every-action controlled restart:** first add deterministic settled and deferred production-like Test→Test / Play→Play reproductions around UI intent, lifecycle generation, media leases, audio source/clock, and gameplay generation. Replace the proven `menuStarting` intent drop and adjacent unawaited playback race with one generation-owned serialized action-intent queue. Every accepted Play/Test action performs complete stale-safe teardown, zero seek, exact selected-content configuration, purpose-resource reacquisition, `gameplay.requestStart`, and awaited initial audio commit. UI actions remain available for intentional restart; stale generations cannot pause/release the newest run.
- **Beat Saber timing authority:** promote exact Info.dat `noteJumpMovementSpeed` and `noteJumpStartBeatOffset` through authoring request/worker/converter into a hash-bound `aerobeat.song-package.v5` source timing record. Pin algorithm identity and constants: starting HJD `4`, threshold `17.999`, pre-offset base floor `1`, final minimum `.25`, and exact operation order. Validate/recompute BPM, NJS, offset, HJD, reaction time, and diagnostic Beat Saber jump-distance meters at authoring and content load. Repair the latent `3c9d` Hard false oracle from offset `0` to exact `+1`; reject malformed camel/legacy field masking rather than coercing it to zero. Reject v4.1 relative-NJS-event maps with a stable unsupported code in this first slice rather than silently flattening dynamic events.
- **Direct AeroBeat mapping:** default spawn lead is Beat Saber reaction time, not Beat Saber meters. AeroBeat world distance is `reactionTimeMs × .006`; `3c9d` Easy/Normal/Hard/Expert therefore spawn at `12` world units and ExpertPlus at `7.2`. The identical selected-difficulty lead applies to normal targets and obstacle leading edges. Obstacles never receive sky or bounce. Missing/invalid v5 timing fails closed as reimport-required unless the valid AeroBeat override is enabled; do not silently synthesize community fallback values.
- **Strict Game Setup v2:** atomically migrate only valid v1 grid state. Persist independent booleans for Arrival group numbers, Attention halo, Next-up ribbon, and Nose camera parallax, all default `false`; persist a spawn override `{enabled:false, normalSpawnDistanceWorldUnits:50}`; persist configurable horizontal/vertical camera ranges initially `.55/.35` world units. Invalid/newer records reset atomically. These trusted-child settings never enter public snapshots, messages, network traffic, or package hashes.
- **Stable timing groups:** index exact finite `centerTimestampMs` equality once per content/session generation. Actionable notes, punches, and guards receive immutable one-based absolute group ordinals; simultaneous chord members and both halves of a guard share one group. Bombs, walls, squats, and weaves neither consume ordinals nor receive cues. Never renumber on cull, hit, miss, pause, or seek. The halo ladder covers at most the next three unresolved groups; the ribbon marks the earliest unresolved group. All cues are neutral/additive and never replace selected-song colors.
- **Full nose-camera experiment:** derive private measured-only nose deflection from the averaged calibration baseline. Keep world targets and gameplay collision canonical; translate only the production camera in X/Y and project targets and markers through that same active camera in the same tick. Initial bounds: X `.55`, Y `.35` world units; axial dead zone `.04`; smoothing `120 ms`; max speed X/Y `3/2` world units/s; recenter `180 ms`; stale boundary `150 ms`. Preserve Z/rotation/FOV/clips. Mirroring is owned upstream; athlete `+X→camera +X`, athlete screen-down `+Y→camera −Y`. Camera Play while visible/menu-closed/calibrated/playing is the only active state. Test, no-camera, calibration, countdown, pause, menu, hidden, terminal, stale/source change, detach, and context loss recenter or reset safely. No raw nose/baseline/offset data is retained or exposed.

### Proposed executable phases after approval

1. **Red-path diagnostics and strict contracts:** add failing reproductions for same-purpose restart races and the rejected miss/default behavior; define package v5 source timing and Game Setup v2 schemas, migration, privacy boundaries, and exact fixtures.
2. **Parallel foundation:** (a) thread source NJS/offset and derived timing through vendor/authoring/content; (b) expose a private measured parallax sample in input; (c) add immutable assembly arrival-group projection; (d) repair renderer mode defaults/miss trajectory/above-label behavior; (e) implement generation-owned serialized Play/Test restart admission.
3. **Visual experiment integration:** implement renderer config/layers for three independent timing cues; implement bounded production-camera translation and same-camera marker projection; wire accessible ≥44 px Game Setup controls, Test JSON, persisted spawn override, source timing summary, and all lifecycle resets.
4. **Coder verification:** each owning repo runs focused unit/contract tests, strict exact-shape/hash fixtures, direct/iframe browser matrices, real `3c9d` all-difficulty timing checks, miss boundary tables, restart race matrices, visual pixels, accessibility, privacy, disposal/context recovery, and performance checks; each intentional repo change is committed/pushed without touching immutable releases.
5. **Independent QA:** a separate QA role validates all modes and all toggle combinations, selected-song colors, chords/guards/hazards, target-marker coincidence at camera corners, no-camera parity, real media same-action restarts, obstacle alignment, storage migration, malformed input fail-closed behavior, and zero browser noise.
6. **Independent audit:** a separate auditor checks exact plan/Bead/diff/commit/push state, source→runtime timing provenance, privacy exclusions, raw immutability, prior evidence preservation, and release readiness. Findings return to new repair Beads; no release is built before source audit PASS.

8. **Physical and performance gate:** Derrick toggles each guidance treatment and parallax on the target device, retests Flow/Lanes/Grid/misses/restarts/spawn/markers/terminal behavior, and supplies explicit PASS or revisions. Run the authoritative Bug A/B/C/C/B/A profile against the accepted successor. Only explicit Derrick PASS permits final Bead closure and plan/Git wrap-up.
